#include &quot;vm_interpreted_light.hpp&quot;
#include &quot;dataset.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	template&lt;class Allocator, bool softAes&gt;
	void InterpretedLightVm&lt;Allocator, softAes&gt;::setCache(randomx_cache* cache) {
		cachePtr &#x3D; cache;
		mem.memory &#x3D; cache-&gt;memory;
	}

	template&lt;class Allocator, bool softAes&gt;
	void InterpretedLightVm&lt;Allocator, softAes&gt;::datasetRead(uint64_t address, int_reg_t(&amp;r)[8]) {
		uint32_t itemNumber &#x3D; address / CacheLineSize;
		int_reg_t rl[8];
		
		initDatasetItem(cachePtr, (uint8_t*)rl, itemNumber);

		for (unsigned q &#x3D; 0; q &lt; 8; ++q)
			r[q] ^&#x3D; rl[q];
	}

	template class InterpretedLightVm&lt;AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;, false&gt;;
	template class InterpretedLightVm&lt;AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;, true&gt;;
	template class InterpretedLightVm&lt;LargePageAllocator, false&gt;;
	template class InterpretedLightVm&lt;LargePageAllocator, true&gt;;
}

#include &lt;new&gt;
#include &quot;allocator.hpp&quot;
#include &quot;intrin_portable.h&quot;
#include &quot;virtual_memory.h&quot;
#include &quot;common.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	template&lt;size_t alignment&gt;
	void* AlignedAllocator&lt;alignment&gt;::allocMemory(size_t count) {
		void *mem &#x3D; rx_aligned_alloc(count, alignment);
		if (mem &#x3D;&#x3D; nullptr)
			throw std::bad_alloc();
		return mem;
	}

	template&lt;size_t alignment&gt;
	void AlignedAllocator&lt;alignment&gt;::freeMemory(void* ptr, size_t count) {
		rx_aligned_free(ptr);
	}

	template struct AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;;

	void* LargePageAllocator::allocMemory(size_t count) {
		void *mem &#x3D; allocLargePagesMemory(count);
		if (mem &#x3D;&#x3D; nullptr)
			throw std::bad_alloc();
		return mem;
	}

	void LargePageAllocator::freeMemory(void* ptr, size_t count) {
		freePagedMemory(ptr, count);
	};

}

#include &lt;stddef.h&gt;
#include &quot;blake2/blake2.h&quot;
#include &quot;blake2/endian.h&quot;
#include &quot;blake2_generator.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	constexpr int maxSeedSize &#x3D; 60;

	Blake2Generator::Blake2Generator(const void* seed, size_t seedSize, int nonce) : dataIndex(sizeof(data)) {
		memset(data, 0, sizeof(data));
		memcpy(data, seed, seedSize &gt; maxSeedSize ? maxSeedSize : seedSize);
		store32(&amp;data[maxSeedSize], nonce);
	}

	uint8_t Blake2Generator::getByte() {
		checkData(1);
		return data[dataIndex++];
	}

	uint32_t Blake2Generator::getUInt32() {
		checkData(4);
		auto ret &#x3D; load32(&amp;data[dataIndex]);
		dataIndex +&#x3D; 4;
		return ret;
	}

	void Blake2Generator::checkData(const size_t bytesNeeded) {
		if (dataIndex + bytesNeeded &gt; sizeof(data)) {
			blake2b(data, sizeof(data), data, sizeof(data), nullptr, 0);
			dataIndex &#x3D; 0;
		}
	}
}

#pragma once

#include &quot;common.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	struct CodeBuffer {
		uint8_t* code;
		int32_t codePos;
		int32_t rcpCount;

		void emit(const uint8_t* src, int32_t len) {
			memcpy(&amp;code[codePos], src, len);
			codePos +&#x3D; len;
		}

		template&lt;typename T&gt;
		void emit(T src) {
			memcpy(&amp;code[codePos], &amp;src, sizeof(src));
			codePos +&#x3D; sizeof(src);
		}

		void emitAt(int32_t codePos, const uint8_t* src, int32_t len) {
			memcpy(&amp;code[codePos], src, len);
		}

		template&lt;typename T&gt;
		void emitAt(int32_t codePos, T src) {
			memcpy(&amp;code[codePos], &amp;src, sizeof(src));
		}
	};

	struct CompilerState : public CodeBuffer {
		int32_t instructionOffsets[RANDOMX_PROGRAM_SIZE];
		int registerUsage[RegistersCount];
	};
}

#if defined(RANDOMX_COMPILER_X86)
#include &quot;jit_compiler_x86.hpp&quot;
#elif defined(RANDOMX_COMPILER_A64)
#include &quot;jit_compiler_a64.hpp&quot;
#elif defined(RANDOMX_COMPILER_RV64)
#include &quot;jit_compiler_rv64.hpp&quot;
#else
#include &quot;jit_compiler_fallback.hpp&quot;
#endif

#if defined(__OpenBSD__) || defined(__NetBSD__) || (defined(__APPLE__) &amp;&amp; defined(__aarch64__))
#define RANDOMX_FORCE_SECURE
#endif

#pragma once

#include &lt;cstdint&gt;
#include &quot;instruction.hpp&quot;
#include &quot;common.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	class SuperscalarProgram {
	public:
		Instruction&amp; operator()(int pc) {
			return programBuffer[pc];
		}
		friend std::ostream&amp; operator&lt;&lt;(std::ostream&amp; os, const SuperscalarProgram&amp; p) {
			p.print(os);
			return os;
		}
		uint32_t getSize() {
			return size;
		}
		void setSize(uint32_t val) {
			size &#x3D; val;
		}
		int getAddressRegister() {
			return addrReg;
		}
		void setAddressRegister(int val) {
			addrReg &#x3D; val;
		}

		Instruction programBuffer[SuperscalarMaxSize];
		uint32_t size
#ifndef NDEBUG
			&#x3D; 0
#endif
			;
		int addrReg;
		double ipc;
		int codeSize;
		int macroOps;
		int decodeCycles;
		int cpuLatency;
		int asicLatency;
		int mulCount;
		int cpuLatencies[8];
		int asicLatencies[8];
	private:
		void print(std::ostream&amp; os) const {
			for (unsigned i &#x3D; 0; i &lt; size; ++i) {
				auto instr &#x3D; programBuffer[i];
				os &lt;&lt; instr;
			}
		}
	};

}

#pragma once

#include &lt;cstdint&gt;
#include &lt;ostream&gt;
#include &quot;common.hpp&quot;
#include &quot;instruction.hpp&quot;
#include &quot;blake2/endian.h&quot;

namespace randomx {

	struct ProgramConfiguration {
		uint64_t eMask[2];
		uint32_t readReg0, readReg1, readReg2, readReg3;
	};

	class Program {
	public:
		Instruction&amp; operator()(int pc) {
			return programBuffer[pc];
		}
		friend std::ostream&amp; operator&lt;&lt;(std::ostream&amp; os, const Program&amp; p) {
			p.print(os);
			return os;
		}
		uint64_t getEntropy(int i) {
			return load64(&amp;entropyBuffer[i]);
		}
		uint32_t getSize() {
			return RANDOMX_PROGRAM_SIZE;
		}
	private:
		void print(std::ostream&amp; os) const {
			for (int i &#x3D; 0; i &lt; RANDOMX_PROGRAM_SIZE; ++i) {
				auto instr &#x3D; programBuffer[i];
				os &lt;&lt; instr;
			}
		}
		uint64_t entropyBuffer[16];
		Instruction programBuffer[RANDOMX_PROGRAM_SIZE];
	};

	static_assert(sizeof(Program) % 64 &#x3D;&#x3D; 0, &quot;Invalid size of class randomx::Program&quot;);
}

#pragma once

#include &lt;new&gt;
#include &quot;vm_interpreted.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	template&lt;class Allocator, bool softAes&gt;
	class InterpretedLightVm : public InterpretedVm&lt;Allocator, softAes&gt; {
	public:
		using VmBase&lt;Allocator, softAes&gt;::mem;
		using VmBase&lt;Allocator, softAes&gt;::cachePtr;
		void* operator new(size_t size) {
			void* ptr &#x3D; AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;::allocMemory(size);
			if (ptr &#x3D;&#x3D; nullptr)
				throw std::bad_alloc();
			return ptr;
		}
		void operator delete(void* ptr) {
			AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;::freeMemory(ptr, sizeof(InterpretedLightVm));
		}
		void setDataset(randomx_dataset* dataset) override { }
		void setCache(randomx_cache* cache) override;
	protected:
		void datasetRead(uint64_t address, int_reg_t(&amp;r)[8]) override;
		void datasetPrefetch(uint64_t address) override { }
	};

	using InterpretedLightVmDefault &#x3D; InterpretedLightVm&lt;AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;, true&gt;;
	using InterpretedLightVmHardAes &#x3D; InterpretedLightVm&lt;AlignedAllocator&lt;CacheLineSize&gt;, false&gt;;
	using InterpretedLightVmLargePage &#x3D; InterpretedLightVm&lt;LargePageAllocator, true&gt;;
	using InterpretedLightVmLargePageHardAes &#x3D; InterpretedLightVm&lt;LargePageAllocator, false&gt;;
}

#pragma once

#include &lt;stdint.h&gt;
#include &quot;intrin_portable.h&quot;

rx_vec_i128 soft_aesenc(rx_vec_i128 in, rx_vec_i128 key);

rx_vec_i128 soft_aesdec(rx_vec_i128 in, rx_vec_i128 key);

template&lt;bool soft&gt;
inline rx_vec_i128 aesenc(rx_vec_i128 in, rx_vec_i128 key) {
	return soft ? soft_aesenc(in, key) : rx_aesenc_vec_i128(in, key);
}

template&lt;bool soft&gt;
inline rx_vec_i128 aesdec(rx_vec_i128 in, rx_vec_i128 key) {
	return soft ? soft_aesdec(in, key) : rx_aesdec_vec_i128(in, key);
}

#pragma once

#include &lt;cstdint&gt;
#include &lt;cstring&gt;
#include &lt;vector&gt;
#include &quot;jit_compiler.hpp&quot;

namespace randomx {

	class Program;
	struct ProgramConfiguration;
	class SuperscalarProgram;
	class Instruction;

	class JitCompilerRV64 {
	public:
		JitCompilerRV64();
		~JitCompilerRV64();
		void generateProgram(Program&amp;, ProgramConfiguration&amp;);
		void generateProgramLight(Program&amp;, ProgramConfiguration&amp;, uint32_t);
		void generateSuperscalarHash(SuperscalarProgram programs[RANDOMX_CACHE_ACCESSES], std::vector&lt;uint64_t&gt;&amp;);
		void generateDatasetInitCode() {}
		ProgramFunc* getProgramFunc() {
			return (ProgramFunc*)entryProgram;
		}
		DatasetInitFunc* getDatasetInitFunc() {
			return (DatasetInitFunc*)entryDataInit;
		}
		uint8_t* getCode() {
			return state.code;
		}
		size_t getCodeSize();
		void enableWriting();
		void enableExecution();
		void enableAll();
	private:
		CompilerState state;
		void* entryDataInit;
		void* entryProgram;
	};
}

// Copyright 2019. The Tari Project

// Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without modification, are permitted provided that the
// following conditions are met:
//
// 1. Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright notice, this list of conditions and the following
// disclaimer.
//
// 2. Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright notice, this list of conditions and the
// following disclaimer in the documentation and/or other materials provided with the distribution.
//
// 3. Neither the name of the copyright holder nor the names of its contributors may be used to endorse or promote
// products derived from this software without specific prior written permission.
//
// THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS AND CONTRIBUTORS &quot;AS IS&quot; AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES,
// INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE ARE
// DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT HOLDER OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL,
// SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF SUBSTITUTE GOODS OR
// SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS INTERRUPTION) HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY,
// WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN ANY WAY OUT OF THE
// USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.

use std::env;

use cmake::Config;

#[allow(clippy::too_many_lines)]
fn main() {
    let randomx_path &#x3D;
        Config::new(env::var(&quot;RANDOMX_DIR&quot;).unwrap_or_else(|_| &quot;RandomX&quot;.to_string())).define(&quot;DARCH&quot;, &quot;native&quot;).build();

    println!(&quot;cargo:rustc-link-search&#x3D;native&#x3D;{}/lib64&quot;, randomx_path.display());
    println!(&quot;cargo:rustc-link-search&#x3D;native&#x3D;{}/lib&quot;, randomx_path.display());
    println!(&quot;cargo:rustc-link-lib&#x3D;static&#x3D;randomx&quot;);
    let target_os &#x3D; env::var(&quot;CARGO_CFG_TARGET_OS&quot;).unwrap_or(&quot;linux&quot;.to_string());
    let dylib_name &#x3D; match target_os.as_str() {
        &quot;freebsd&quot; | &quot;macos&quot; | &quot;ios&quot; &#x3D;&gt; &quot;c++&quot;, // FreeBSD, macOS and iOS use &quot;c++&quot;
        &quot;windows&quot; &#x3D;&gt; &quot;msvcrt&quot;,                // Use MSVC runtime on Windows
        _ &#x3D;&gt; &quot;stdc++&quot;,                        // Default for other systems (Linux, etc.)
    };
    println!(&quot;cargo:rustc-link-lib&#x3D;dylib&#x3D;{}&quot;, dylib_name);

    if cfg!(target_os &#x3D; &quot;windows&quot;) {
        println!(&quot;cargo:rustc-link-lib&#x3D;advapi32&quot;);
    }
}

#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include &quot;../../limbs/limbs.h&quot;

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT_AFFINE;


#endif // OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

// MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR BE LIABLE FOR ANY
// SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize &#x3D; ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &amp;[u8], public_key: &amp;[u8], msg: &amp;[u8]) -&gt; digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx &#x3D; digest::Context::new(&amp;digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature &#x3D; &quot;alloc&quot;)]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize &#x3D; 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -&gt; Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width &#x3D; &quot;64&quot;)]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width &#x3D; &quot;32&quot;)]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 &gt;&gt; crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From&lt;untrusted::EndOfInput&gt; for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -&gt; Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From&lt;core::array::TryFromSliceError&gt; for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -&gt; Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From&lt;KeyRejected&gt; for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -&gt; Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

// SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
// WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
// OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF OR IN
// CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

#[cfg(target_arch &#x3D; &quot;x86&quot;)]
pub fn shift_full_blocks&lt;const BLOCK_LEN: usize&gt;(
    in_out: super::overlapping::Overlapping&lt;&#x27;_, u8&gt;,
    mut transform: impl FnMut(&amp;[u8; BLOCK_LEN]) -&gt; [u8; BLOCK_LEN],
) {
    let (in_out, src) &#x3D; in_out.into_slice_src_mut();
    let in_out_len &#x3D; in_out[src.clone()].len();

    for i in (0..in_out_len).step_by(BLOCK_LEN) {
        let block &#x3D; {
            let input &#x3D;
                &lt;&amp;[u8; BLOCK_LEN]&gt;::try_from(&amp;in_out[(src.start + i)..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
            transform(input)
        };
        let output &#x3D; &lt;&amp;mut [u8; BLOCK_LEN]&gt;::try_from(&amp;mut in_out[i..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
        *output &#x3D; block;
    }
}

/// Polyfill for &#x60;Option::unwrap()&#x60; as a const fn; feature &#x60;const_option&#x60;.
/// https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/67441.
/// TODO(MSRV): Replace this with &#x60;x.unwrap()&#x60;.
///
/// &#x60;T: Copy&#x60; avoids &quot;constant functions cannot evaluate destructors.&quot;
pub const fn unwrap_const&lt;T&gt;(x: Option&lt;T&gt;) -&gt; T
where
    T: Copy,
{
    if let Some(x) &#x3D; x {
        x
    } else {
        panic!(&quot;unwrap_const on &#x60;None&#x60;&quot;);
    }
}

//! Utilities to make dealing with slices less tediuous.

/// Replaces the first N elements of &#x60;a&#x60; with the first N elements of &#x60;b&#x60;, where
/// N is &#x60;core::cmp::min(a.len(), b.len())&#x60;, leaving the rest unchanged.
pub fn overwrite_at_start&lt;T: Copy&gt;(a: &amp;mut [T], b: &amp;[T]) {
    a.iter_mut().zip(b).for_each(|(a, b)| {
        *a &#x3D; *b;
    });
}

use core::{marker::PhantomData, mem::size_of};

/// A ZST that can be added to any type to make the type &#x60;!Send&#x60;.
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct NotSend(PhantomData&lt;*mut ()&gt;);

impl NotSend {
    pub const VALUE: Self &#x3D; Self(PhantomData);
}

#[allow(deprecated)]
const _: () &#x3D; testutil::compile_time_assert_clone::&lt;NotSend&gt;();
#[allow(deprecated)]
const _: () &#x3D; testutil::compile_time_assert_copy::&lt;NotSend&gt;();
const _: () &#x3D; assert!(size_of::&lt;NotSend&gt;() &#x3D;&#x3D; 0);

Preamble

The GNU General Public License is a free, copyleft license for software and other kinds of works.

The licenses for most software and other practical works are designed to take away your freedom to share and change the works. By contrast, the GNU General Public License is intended to guarantee your freedom to share and change all versions of a program--to make sure it remains free software for all its users. We, the Free Software Foundation, use the GNU General Public License for most of our software; it applies also to any other work released this way by its authors. You can apply it to your programs, too.

When we speak of free software, we are referring to freedom, not price. Our General Public Licenses are designed to make sure that you have the freedom to distribute copies of free software (and charge for them if you wish), that you receive source code or can get it if you want it, that you can change the software or use pieces of it in new free programs, and that you know you can do these things.

To protect your rights, we need to prevent others from denying you these rights or asking you to surrender the rights. Therefore, you have certain responsibilities if you distribute copies of the software, or if you modify it: responsibilities to respect the freedom of others.

For example, if you distribute copies of such a program, whether gratis or for a fee, you must pass on to the recipients the same freedoms that you received. You must make sure that they, too, receive or can get the source code. And you must show them these terms so they know their rights.

Developers that use the GNU GPL protect your rights with two steps: (1) assert copyright on the software, and (2) offer you this License giving you legal permission to copy, distribute and/or modify it.

For the developers&#x27; and authors&#x27; protection, the GPL clearly explains that there is no warranty for this free software. For both users&#x27; and authors&#x27; sake, the GPL requires that modified versions be marked as changed, so that their problems will not be attributed erroneously to authors of previous versions.

Some devices are designed to deny users access to install or run modified versions of the software inside them, although the manufacturer can do so. This is fundamentally incompatible with the aim of protecting users&#x27; freedom to change the software. The systematic pattern of such abuse occurs in the area of products for individuals to use, which is precisely where it is most unacceptable. Therefore, we have designed this version of the GPL to prohibit the practice for those products. If such problems arise substantially in other domains, we stand ready to extend this provision to those domains in future versions of the GPL, as needed to protect the freedom of users.

Finally, every program is threatened constantly by software patents. States should not allow patents to restrict development and use of software on general-purpose computers, but in those that do, we wish to avoid the special danger that patents applied to a free program could make it effectively proprietary. To prevent this, the GPL assures that patents cannot be used to render the program non-free.

The precise terms and conditions for copying, distribution and modification follow.

How to Apply These Terms to Your New Programs

If you develop a new program, and you want it to be of the greatest possible use to the public, the best way to achieve this is to make it free software which everyone can redistribute and change under these terms.

To do so, attach the following notices to the program. It is safest to attach them to the start of each source file to most effectively state the exclusion of warranty; and each file should have at least the “copyright” line and a pointer to where the full notice is found.

     &lt;one line to give the program&#x27;s name and a brief idea of what it does.&gt;
     Copyright (C) &lt;year&gt;  &lt;name of author&gt;

     This program is free software: you can redistribute it and/or modify it under the terms of the GNU General Public License as published by the Free Software Foundation, either version 3 of the License, or (at your option) any later version.

     This program is distributed in the hope that it will be useful, but WITHOUT ANY WARRANTY; without even the implied warranty of MERCHANTABILITY or FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE.  See the GNU General Public License for more details.

     You should have received a copy of the GNU General Public License along with this program.  If not, see &lt;http://www.gnu.org/licenses/&gt;.

Also add information on how to contact you by electronic and paper mail.

If the program does terminal interaction, make it output a short notice like this when it starts in an interactive mode:

     &lt;program&gt;  Copyright (C) &lt;year&gt;  &lt;name of author&gt;
     This program comes with ABSOLUTELY NO WARRANTY; for details type &#x60;show w&#x27;.
     This is free software, and you are welcome to redistribute it under certain conditions; type &#x60;show c&#x27; for details.

The hypothetical commands &#x60;show w&#x27; and &#x60;show c&#x27; should show the appropriate parts of the General Public License. Of course, your program&#x27;s commands might be different; for a GUI interface, you would use an “about box”.

You should also get your employer (if you work as a programmer) or school, if any, to sign a “copyright disclaimer” for the program, if necessary. For more information on this, and how to apply and follow the GNU GPL, see &lt;http://www.gnu.org/licenses/&gt;.

The GNU General Public License does not permit incorporating your program into proprietary programs. If your program is a subroutine library, you may consider it more useful to permit linking proprietary applications with the library. If this is what you want to do, use the GNU Lesser General Public License instead of this License. But first, please read &lt;http://www.gnu.org/philosophy/why-not-lgpl.html&gt;.
